This split keeps the constructor cheap\n    and matches the cliEval orchestrator's \"build many drivers, spawn them in\n    parallel\" pattern.\n\n    Parameters\n    ----------\n    command:\n        Either a single executable string or a ``[command, *args]`` list.\n        Mirrors the ``pexpect.spawn`` first-argument contract.\n    env:\n        Optional environment overlay. ``None`` inherits the parent process\n        environment. To run with the per-eval HOME, pass\n        ``HomeIsolation.env()`` here.\n    cwd:\n        Working directory for the child. ``None`` inherits the parent CWD.\n    prompt_ready_pattern:\n        Regex matched against the PTY output by\n        :meth:`expect_prompt_ready`. Defaults to\n        :data:`DEFAULT_PROMPT_READY_PATTERN`.\n    default_timeout:\n        Wall-clock seconds used by ``expect_prompt_ready`` and ``wait_exit``\n        when the caller does not specify a per-call timeout.\n    encoding:\n        Text encoding used by ``pexpect.spawn``. ``\"utf-8\"`` is the default;\n        pass ``None`` to receive raw ``bytes`` (rarely useful for Claude Code,\n        whose TTY output is text-mode UTF-8).\n    dimensions:\n        ``(rows, cols)`` of the simulated terminal. The default\n        ``(40, 120)`` matches Claude Code's recommended dev-host geometry.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        command: str | list[str],\n        *,\n        env: dict[str, str] | None = None,\n        cwd: Path | str | None = None,\n        prompt_ready_pattern: str | Pattern[str] = DEFAULT_PROMPT_READY_PATTERN,\n        default_timeout: float = 30.0,\n        encoding: str | None = \"utf-8\",\n        dimensions: tuple[int, int] = (40, 120),\n    ) -> None:\n        if isinstance(command, str):\n            self._argv: list[str] = [command]\n        else:\n            if not command:\n                raise ValueError(\"command must be a non-empty string or list\")\n            self._argv = list(command)\n\n        self._env: dict[str, str] | None = dict(env) if env is not None else None\n        self._cwd: str | None = str(cwd) if cwd is not None else None\n\n        if isinstance(prompt_ready_pattern, str):\n            self._prompt_ready_pattern: Pattern[str] = re.compile(prompt_ready_pattern)\n        else:\n            self._prompt_ready_pattern = prompt_ready_pattern\n\n        if default_timeout <= 0:\n            raise ValueError(\"default_timeout must be > 0\")\n        self._default_timeout = float(default_timeout)\n        self._encoding = encoding\n        self._dimensions = dimensions\n\n        self._child: Any | None = None  # pexpect.spawn instance once spawned\n        self._exit_code: int | None = None\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ lifecycle\n\n    def spawn(self) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Launch the child subprocess via ``pexpect.spawn``.\n\n        Idempotent: a second call after the child has exited starts a fresh\n        process; a second call while the child is still alive raises\n        :class:`PtyDriverError`.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        if self._child is not None and self.is_alive():\n            raise PtyDriverError(\"PtyDriver.spawn called while child is still alive\")\n\n        rows, cols = self._dimensions\n        argv0, *args = self._argv\n        self._child = _pexpect.spawn(\n            argv0,\n            args=args,\n            env=self._env,\n            cwd=self._cwd,\n            timeout=self._default_timeout,\n            encoding=self._encoding,\n            dimensions=(rows, cols),\n        )\n        self._exit_code = None\n\n    def __enter__(self) -> \"PtyDriver\":\n        self.spawn()\n        return self\n\n    def __exit__(self, exc_type, exc, tb) -> None:\n        try:\n            if self.is_alive():\n                self.terminate()\n        finally:\n            self.close()\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ state\n\n    def is_alive(self) -> bool:\n        \"\"\"Return ``True`` while the child is running.\"\"\"\n\n        return self._child is not None and bool(self._child.isalive())\n\n    @property\n    def pid(self) -> int | None:\n        \"\"\"PID of the child process, or ``None`` if not yet spawned.\"\"\"\n\n        if self._child is None:\n            return None\n        return int(self._child.pid)\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ I/O\n\n    def _require_child(self) -> Any:\n        if self._child is None:\n            raise PtyDriverNotStarted(\"spawn() must be called before interaction\")\n        return self._child\n\n    def expect_prompt_ready(self, timeout: float | None = None) -> str:\n        \"\"\"Block until the child emits the prompt-ready indicator.\n\n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        timeout:\n            Wall-clock seconds. ``None`` uses the driver's\n            ``default_timeout``. Pass an explicit value to override on a\n            per-call basis.\n\n        Returns\n        -------\n        str\n            The bytes captured *before* the prompt-ready match. Useful when\n            the caller wants to inspect banner / version output the child\n            emitted prior to becoming interactive.\n\n        Raises\n        ------\n        PtyDriverTimeout\n            The prompt-ready pattern did not appear within ``timeout``\n            seconds.\n        PtyDriverEOF\n            The child closed its PTY (typically a crash) before emitting\n            the prompt-ready pattern.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        child = self._require_child()\n        effective_timeout = self._default_timeout if timeout is None else float(timeout)\n        try:\n            child.expect(self._prompt_ready_pattern, timeout=effective_timeout)\n        except _pexpect.TIMEOUT as exc:\n            raise PtyDriverTimeout(\n                f\"prompt_ready_pattern not seen within {effective_timeout:.2f}s\"\n            ) from exc\n        except _pexpect.EOF as exc:\n            raise PtyDriverEOF(\n                \"child closed PTY before prompt-ready pattern matched\"\n            ) from exc\n        before = child.before\n        return before if isinstance(before, str) else (before or b\"\").decode(\n            self._encoding or \"utf-8\", errors=\"replace\"\n        )\n\n    def inject_prompt(self, text: str) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Send a user-style prompt to the child, terminated by ``\\\\r\\\\n``.\n\n        Wraps ``pexpect.spawn.send`` with the carriage-return + line-feed\n        sequence Claude Code expects from a real TTY. The send is flushed\n        before returning.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        child = self._require_child()\n        if not isinstance(text, str):\n            raise TypeError(\"inject_prompt requires str (use write_stdin for bytes)\")\n        child.send(text + \"\\r\\n\")\n        # pexpect.spawn writes via os.write which is unbuffered, but be explicit\n        # in case a subclass or vendored fork swaps the I/O layer.\n        flush = getattr(child, \"flush\", None)\n        if callable(flush):\n            try:\n                flush()\n            except (OSError, ValueError):\n                pass\n\n    def write_stdin(self, data: str | bytes) -> int:\n        \"\"\"Raw write to the child's stdin.\n\n        Returns the number of bytes (or characters when ``encoding`` is set)\n        that pexpect reports it wrote. No newline is appended; callers that\n        want a prompt-style write should use :meth:`inject_prompt`.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        child = self._require_child()\n        if isinstance(data, bytes) and self._encoding is not None:\n            data = data.decode(self._encoding, errors=\"strict\")\n        result = child.send(data)\n        return int(result) if result is not None else len(data)\n\n    def read_stdout(self, size: int = -1, timeout: float | None = None) -> str:\n        \"\"\"Non-blocking read of up to ``size`` bytes from the child PTY.\n\n        ``size`` is the maximum chunk size; negative values map to \"as much\n        as the PTY currently holds, capped at 65536 bytes.\" Returns an empty\n        string when no data is currently buffered (instead of blocking).\n\n        ``timeout`` is the maximum time pexpect will wait for the first byte.\n        ``None`` uses the driver's ``default_timeout``; pass ``0`` for a true\n        non-blocking poll.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        child = self._require_child()\n        cap = 65536 if size is None or size < 0 else int(size)\n        if cap <= 0:\n            return \"\"\n        wait = self._default_timeout if timeout is None else float(timeout)\n        try:\n            chunk = child.read_nonblocking(size=cap, timeout=wait)\n        except _pexpect.TIMEOUT:\n            return \"\"\n        except _pexpect.EOF:\n            return \"\"\n        if isinstance(chunk, bytes):\n            chunk = chunk.decode(self._encoding or \"utf-8\", errors=\"replace\")\n        return chunk\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ exit\n\n    def wait_exit(self, timeout: float | None = None) -> int:\n        \"\"\"Wait for the child to exit and return its exit code.\n\n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        timeout:\n            Wall-clock seconds. ``None`` uses the driver's default timeout.\n\n        Returns\n        -------\n        int\n            POSIX exit code. Signal-terminated children return a negative\n            value (``-SIGTERM`` = ``-15``, etc.) so callers can distinguish\n            ``exit(124)`` from ``SIGTERM`` cleanly.\n\n        Raises\n        ------\n        PtyDriverTimeout\n            The child was still alive after ``timeout`` seconds. The child\n            remains running; call :meth:`terminate` to clean it up.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        child = self._require_child()\n        if self._exit_code is not None and not child.isalive():\n            return self._exit_code\n\n        effective_timeout = self._default_timeout if timeout is None else float(timeout)\n        # pexpect.wait() doesn't accept a timeout; emulate one by polling\n        # ``expect(EOF, timeout=...)`` which blocks until the PTY closes.\n        try:\n            child.expect(_pexpect.EOF, timeout=effective_timeout)\n        except _pexpect.TIMEOUT as exc:\n            raise PtyDriverTimeout(\n                f\"child did not exit within {effective_timeout:.2f}s\"\n            ) from exc\n\n        # Wait reaps the zombie and populates exitstatus / signalstatus.\n        try:\n            child.wait()\n        except _pexpect.ExceptionPexpect:\n            # ``wait()`` raises if pexpect believes the child is still alive;\n            # in our flow EOF already arrived, so any residual race is\n            # benign -- isalive() will return False and exitstatus / signalstatus\n            # will be set on the next attribute read.\n            pass\n\n        if child.signalstatus is not None:\n            self._exit_code = -int(child.signalstatus)\n        else:\n            self._exit_code = int(child.exitstatus) if child.exitstatus is not None else -1\n        return self._exit_code\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ cleanup\n\n    def terminate(self, force: bool = False) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Send SIGTERM (or SIGKILL when ``force=True``) to the child.\n\n        Safe to call when the child has already exited.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        if self._child is None or not self._child.isalive():\n            return\n        self._child.terminate(force=force)\n\n    def close(self) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Close the PTY file descriptors and release pexpect's bookkeeping.\"\"\"\n\n        if self._child is None:\n            return\n        try:\n            self._child.close(force=False)\n        except _pexpect.ExceptionPexpect:\n            # close() can raise when the child is still alive; force-close to\n            # avoid leaking the PTY fd. terminate() is safe to call twice.\n            try:\n                self._child.close(force=True)\n            except _pexpect.ExceptionPexpect:\n                pass\n        if self._child.signalstatus is not None:\n            self._exit_code = -int(self._child.signalstatus)\n        elif self._child.exitstatus is not None and self._exit_code is None:\n            self._exit_code = int(self._child.exitstatus)\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ helpers\n\n    @property\n    def exit_code(self) -> int | None:\n        \"\"\"Last-captured exit code (``None`` until the child has exited).\"\"\"\n\n        return self._exit_code\n\n    def fileno(self) -> int:\n        \"\"\"Return the PTY's read file descriptor for ``select``-based loops.\"\"\"\n\n        return int(self._require_child().fileno())\n","numLines":426,"startLine":1,"totalLines":426}}}

This is the FR-G1 \"real subprocess discipline\" load-bearing\n19\tcomponent: every eval body that needs to interact with `claude` does so\n20\tthrough this class, and every `claude --help`-style smoke check the harness\n21\temits during `eval doctor` flows through the same code path.\n22\t\n23\t## Driver contract\n24\t\n25\t### Constructor\n26\t\n27\t```python\n28\tPtyDriver(\n29\t    command: str | list[str],\n30\t    *,\n31\t    env: dict[str, str] | None = None,\n32\t    cwd: Path | str | None = None,\n33\t    prompt_ready_pattern: str | re.Pattern[str] = DEFAULT_PROMPT_READY_PATTERN,\n34\t    default_timeout: float = 30.0,\n35\t    encoding: str | None = \"utf-8\",\n36\t    dimensions: tuple[int, int] = (40, 120),\n37\t) -> None\n38\t```\n39\t\n40\tThe constructor validates arguments and stores them. It does **not** spawn\n41\tthe child — that happens on `spawn()` or `__enter__`. This split keeps\n42\tconstruction cheap and matches the cliEval orchestrator's \"build many\n43\tdrivers, spawn them in parallel\" pattern (T03.16).\n44\t\n45\t### COMP-007 method set (T02.16 AC bullet 1)\n46\t\n47\t| Method                                 | Behavior                                                                 |\n48\t|----------------------------------------|--------------------------------------------------------------------------|\n49\t| `expect_prompt_ready(timeout=None)`    | Block until the prompt-ready pattern matches; return PTY buffer captured *before* the match. |\n50\t| `inject_prompt(text: str)`             | `child.send(text + \"\\r\\n\")` then flush — Claude-Code-idiomatic write.    |\n51\t| `write_stdin(data: str \\| bytes) -> int` | Raw write to the child's stdin; **no** newline appended.                  |\n52\t| `read_stdout(size=-1, timeout=None)`   | Non-blocking read of up to `size` bytes from the PTY; returns `\"\"` when idle. |\n53\t| `wait_exit(timeout=None) -> int`       | Wait for the child to exit; return its exit code.                        |\n54\t\n55\tTwo error classes carry timeout / EOF signals up to the caller:\n56\t\n57\t* `PtyDriverTimeout` — raised by `expect_prompt_ready` and `wait_exit` when\n58\t  the timeout budget elapses with the child still alive (and pattern still\n59\t  unseen).\n60\t* `PtyDriverEOF` — raised by `expect_prompt_ready` when the child closes\n61\t  its PTY (typically a crash) before emitting the prompt-ready pattern.\n62\t\n63\tA `PtyDriverNotStarted` guard fires when any I/O method is called before\n64\t`spawn()`. `PtyDriverError` is the shared base, so callers can `except\n65\tPtyDriverError:` to catch the entire surface.\n66\t\n67\t### Default prompt-ready pattern (DEFAULT_PROMPT_READY_PATTERN)\n68\t\n69\t```python\n70\tDEFAULT_PROMPT_READY_PATTERN: str = r\"[>$] *\\r*\\n\"\n71\t```\n72\t\n73\tMatches a bare `>` or `$` followed by optional whitespace and a CR/LF\n74\tterminator. The pattern relies on the newline terminator rather than the\n75\t`$` end-of-string anchor because `pexpect` compiles patterns without the\n76\tMULTILINE flag and its incremental PTY buffer almost always has trailing\n77\tCR/LF after the marker — an unanchored match against the newline is both\n78\tmore permissive and more reliable. Callers that need a binary-specific\n79\tpattern (e.g. when the real `claude` REPL emits a `╭─` framed prompt)\n80\toverride `prompt_ready_pattern` at construction.\n81\t\n82\t### Exit-code semantics (T02.16 AC bullet 3)\n83\t\n84\t`wait_exit` returns:\n85\t\n86\t* `int >= 0` — POSIX exit code from `child.exitstatus`.\n87\t* `int < 0`  — POSIX-convention `-SIGNUM` when the child was killed by a\n88\t  signal (signal code lifted from `child.signalstatus`).\n89\t\n90\tThe negative-on-signal contract lets callers distinguish `exit(124)`\n91\t(timeout exit) from `SIGTERM` cleanly — important because the orchestrator\n92\t(T03.x) maps signal termination and explicit `exit(124)` to different\n93\toutcome statuses (`INTERRUPTED` vs `TIMEOUT`).\n94\t\n95\t`wait_exit` is idempotent after the child has exited: the captured exit\n96\tcode is cached on `_exit_code` and returned on subsequent calls without\n97\tre-driving `expect(EOF)`.\n98\t\n99\t### Lifecycle hygiene\n100\t\n101\t* `is_alive() -> bool` — `pexpect.spawn.isalive()` proxy.\n102\t* `terminate(force: bool = False)` — SIGTERM by default, SIGKILL when\n103\t  `force=True`. Safe to call on an already-exited child.\n104\t* `close()` — close the PTY fd and force-close on `pexpect.ExceptionPexpect`\n105\t  so we never leak the fd. Also pulls the exit code one last time so\n106\t  callers that go straight to `close()` (no `wait_exit`) still get\n107\t  `driver.exit_code` populated.\n108\t* `__enter__` / `__exit__` — context-manager support: enters with a fresh\n109\t  spawn, exits with `terminate()` + `close()`. Used by every interactive\n110\t  test in `tests/cli/eval/test_pty_driver.py`.\n111\t\n112\t### Import strategy\n113\t\n114\t```python\n115\ttry:\n116\t    from superclaude.cli.eval.pty import pexpect as _pexpect  # vendored\n117\texcept ImportError:\n118\t    import pexpect as _pexpect                                # fallback\n119\t```\n120\t\n121\tThe vendored path is the long-term home (NFR-MAINT1 / T02.01). The\n122\tfall-back keeps the module importable while T02.01's vendored sources are\n123\tstill landing. This is the FR-G1 \"real subprocess discipline\" load-bearing\ncomponent: every eval body that needs to interact with `claude` does so\nthrough this class, and every `claude --help`-style smoke check the harness\nemits during `eval doctor` flows through the same code path.\n\n## Driver contract\n\n### Constructor\n\n```python\nPtyDriver(\n    command: str | list[str],\n    *,\n    env: dict[str, str] | None = None,\n    cwd: Path | str | None = None,\n    prompt_ready_pattern: str | re.Pattern[str] = DEFAULT_PROMPT_READY_PATTERN,\n    default_timeout: float = 30.0,\n    encoding: str | None = \"utf-8\",\n    dimensions: tuple[int, int] = (40, 120),\n) -> None\n```\n\nThe constructor validates arguments and stores them. It does **not** spawn\nthe child — that happens on `spawn()` or `__enter__`. This split keeps\nconstruction cheap and matches the cliEval orchestrator's \"build many\ndrivers, spawn them in parallel\" pattern (T03.16).\n\n### COMP-007 method set (T02.16 AC bullet 1)\n\n| Method                                 | Behavior                                                                 |\n|----------------------------------------|--------------------------------------------------------------------------|\n| `expect_prompt_ready(timeout=None)`    | Block until the prompt-ready pattern matches; return PTY buffer captured *before* the match. |\n| `inject_prompt(text: str)`             | `child.send(text + \"\\r\\n\")` then flush — Claude-Code-idiomatic write.    |\n| `write_stdin(data: str \\| bytes) -> int` | Raw write to the child's stdin; **no** newline appended.                  |\n| `read_stdout(size=-1, timeout=None)`   | Non-blocking read of up to `size` bytes from the PTY; returns `\"\"` when idle. |\n| `wait_exit(timeout=None) -> int`       | Wait for the child to exit; return its exit code.                        |\n\nTwo error classes carry timeout / EOF signals up to the caller:\n\n* `PtyDriverTimeout` — raised by `expect_prompt_ready` and `wait_exit` when\n  the timeout budget elapses with the child still alive (and pattern still\n  unseen).\n* `PtyDriverEOF` — raised by `expect_prompt_ready` when the child closes\n  its PTY (typically a crash) before emitting the prompt-ready pattern.\n\nA `PtyDriverNotStarted` guard fires when any I/O method is called before\n`spawn()`. `PtyDriverError` is the shared base, so callers can `except\nPtyDriverError:` to catch the entire surface.\n\n### Default prompt-ready pattern (DEFAULT_PROMPT_READY_PATTERN)\n\n```python\nDEFAULT_PROMPT_READY_PATTERN: str = r\"[>$] *\\r*\\n\"\n```\n\nMatches a bare `>` or `$` followed by optional whitespace and a CR/LF\nterminator. The pattern relies on the newline terminator rather than the\n`$` end-of-string anchor because `pexpect` compiles patterns without the\nMULTILINE flag and its incremental PTY buffer almost always has trailing\nCR/LF after the marker — an unanchored match against the newline is both\nmore permissive and more reliable. Callers that need a binary-specific\npattern (e.g. when the real `claude` REPL emits a `╭─` framed prompt)\noverride `prompt_ready_pattern` at construction.\n\n### Exit-code semantics (T02.16 AC bullet 3)\n\n`wait_exit` returns:\n\n* `int >= 0` — POSIX exit code from `child.exitstatus`.\n* `int < 0`  — POSIX-convention `-SIGNUM` when the child was killed by a\n  signal (signal code lifted from `child.signalstatus`).\n\nThe negative-on-signal contract lets callers distinguish `exit(124)`\n(timeout exit) from `SIGTERM` cleanly — important because the orchestrator\n(T03.x) maps signal termination and explicit `exit(124)` to different\noutcome statuses (`INTERRUPTED` vs `TIMEOUT`).\n\n`wait_exit` is idempotent after the child has exited: the captured exit\ncode is cached on `_exit_code` and returned on subsequent calls without\nre-driving `expect(EOF)`.\n\n### Lifecycle hygiene\n\n* `is_alive() -> bool` — `pexpect.spawn.isalive()` proxy.\n* `terminate(force: bool = False)` — SIGTERM by default, SIGKILL when\n  `force=True`. Safe to call on an already-exited child.\n* `close()` — close the PTY fd and force-close on `pexpect.ExceptionPexpect`\n  so we never leak the fd. Also pulls the exit code one last time so\n  callers that go straight to `close()` (no `wait_exit`) still get\n  `driver.exit_code` populated.\n* `__enter__` / `__exit__` — context-manager support: enters with a fresh\n  spawn, exits with `terminate()` + `close()`. Used by every interactive\n  test in `tests/cli/eval/test_pty_driver.py`.\n\n### Import strategy\n\n```python\ntry:\n    from superclaude.cli.eval.pty import pexpect as _pexpect  # vendored\nexcept ImportError:\n    import pexpect as _pexpect                                # fallback\n```\n\nThe vendored path is the long-term home (NFR-MAINT1 / T02.01). The\nfall-back keeps the module importable while T02.01's vendored sources are\nstill landing. Once T02.01 ships, the fall-back becomes dead code — this\nis intentional, not a long-term escape hatch.\n\n## Acceptance criteria mapping (T02.16)\n\n| AC bullet                                                                                                          | Evidence                                                                                                                                                  |\n|---|---|\n| `PtyDriver` in `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty_driver.py` exposes the 5 methods.                                     | `test_method_surface_matches_comp_007_contract` — introspects `inspect.getmembers(PtyDriver, predicate=inspect.isfunction)` and asserts the five names.    |\n| A unit test spawns a real `claude --help` (or test-stub) subprocess via PTY and `expect_prompt_ready()` returns.   | `test_expect_prompt_ready_returns_before_timeout_against_stub` (always runs) + `test_real_claude_help_smoketest` (opt-in; skipped when binary absent).    |\n| `wait_exit()` captures and returns the subprocess exit code accurately.                                            | `test_wait_exit_captures_exit_code_from_stub[0,1,42,124]` parametrized + `test_wait_exit_reports_signal_termination_as_negative` + idempotency test.       |\n\nThe four extra test groups beyond the three AC bullets are defense in\ndepth — every public method has at least one positive test and one\nfailure-mode test, and the lifecycle (`spawn`/`terminate`/`close`) is\ncovered by the context-manager round-trips that wrap every interactive\ntest.\n\n## FR-G1 satisfaction\n\nThe cliEval roadmap's FR-G1 (\"real subprocess discipline\") forbids any\nin-process Anthropic SDK path under `cli/eval/`. `PtyDriver` satisfies the\nconstructive half of that requirement by being the sole entry point for\nspawning a `claude` subprocess in the harness:\n\n1. The constructor accepts `command: str | list[str]`; the cliEval\n   orchestrator passes `[\"claude\", *args]` exclusively.\n2. The spawn path is `pexpect.spawn` — a literal POSIX `fork` + `execve`\n   inside a fresh PTY. There is no Python-level shortcut that bypasses the\n   binary.\n3. Per-eval isolation is applied through `env=HomeIsolation.env()` — the\n   driver does not look at `os.environ` directly, so a forgotten env\n   override cannot leak the host's `$HOME` into the child.\n\nThe negative half (\"no SDK imports under `cli/eval/`\") is enforced by the\nban-import lint rule landed in T03.x (`COMP-013` / D-0038 family). The two\ntogether close FR-G1.\n\n## Out-of-scope (delegated downstream)\n\n* ANSI stripping + line buffering → T02.17 `PtyStream` / D-0037.\n* `claude` version pinning + capability discovery → `eval doctor`\n  subcommand and `CapabilityGates` (Phase 1).\n* SIGINT cancellation + timeout reaping → T03.x `EvalRunner` (calls\n  `terminate(force=True)` then `wait_exit`).\n\nEach of these consumes `PtyDriver` rather than reaching into pexpect\ndirectly — the driver is the single concentration point for the PTY\ncontract.\n","numLines":171,"startLine":1,"totalLines":171}}}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-7","id":"msg_01Pifg6mbfYCivyk7BCiwyRJ","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01VEMqeKrY6EEX6ZHKsQuSzp","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/config/workspace/IronClaude/src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty_stream.py","content":"\"\"\"COMP-011 PtyStream -- ANSI-stripped, line-buffered iteration over a PTY.\n\nThe cliEval harness drives the real ``claude`` binary through a pseudo-terminal\n(see :mod:`superclaude.cli.eval.pty_driver`). The raw PTY output is a stream\nof bytes peppered with ANSI escape sequences (colour codes, cursor moves,\ntitle-set OSC bursts) and partial lines: ``read_stdout`` may return half a\nline on one call and the rest on the next. Downstream ``Expect.stdout`` /\n``Expect.stderr`` primitives (T04.07) need to assert against *plain text\nlines*, not raw chunks, so this module sits between the driver and those\nprimitives.\n\nResponsibilities\n----------------\n\n* **ANSI stripping.** Every chunk passes through :data:`ANSI_ESCAPE_RE`\n  before reaching the line buffer. CSI sequences (colour / SGR / cursor),\n  OSC sequences (title-set, hyperlinks), and 7-bit C1 singletons are all\n  removed. The regex is intentionally permissive: stripping too much is\n  preferable to leaking escape bytes into ``Expect`` assertions, where a\n  hidden ``\\\\x1B[0m`` reset would silently break a substring check.\n* **Line buffering.** Chunks are accumulated in an internal buffer; the\n  iterator only yields when a ``\\\\n`` terminator arrives. Partial lines do\n  not appear as empty strings -- they wait for completion or for an\n  explicit :meth:`drain` flush.\n* **Timeout.** Each :meth:`read_line` call carries a wall-clock budget. If\n  the budget elapses with no complete line in the buffer, :class:`PtyTimeout`\n  is raised. This is the stall signal the orchestrator's per-eval reaper\n  watches for -- a hung child that prints a colour reset and nothing else\n  must not look like progress to the harness.\n\nAPI contract (T02.17 / D-0037 acceptance criteria)\n--------------------------------------------------\n\n```python\nstream = PtyStream(driver, timeout=5.0)\nfor line in stream:\n    if \"ready\" in line:\n        break\n# or, explicitly:\nline = stream.read_line(timeout=2.0)        # str, ANSI-stripped, no trailing \\\\n\ntail = stream.drain()                       # whatever sits in the buffer\n```\n\nThe constructor accepts either a :class:`PtyDriver` (the standard case --\n``read_stdout`` is the chunk producer) or any callable ``(timeout: float)\n-> str`` returning the next available chunk. The callable form keeps the\nunit tests independent of pexpect.\n\nFailure modes\n-------------\n\n* :class:`PtyTimeout` -- raised by :meth:`read_line` (and propagated from\n  :meth:`__next__`) when the per-line budget elapses with no terminator.\n* :class:`PtyStreamError` -- raised when the stream is used after\n  :meth:`close`. Also serves as the shared base class for\n  ``except PtyStreamError:`` callers.\n\nThe iterator deliberately propagates :class:`PtyTimeout` rather than\nswallowing it into :class:`StopIteration`. Downstream ``Expect`` primitives\ntreat a stalled stream as a deterministic failure, not as \"stream\nexhausted\" -- collapsing both into ``StopIteration`` would silently mask a\nhung child during an assertion loop.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport re\nimport time\nfrom typing import Any, Callable, Pattern\n\n__all__ = [\n    \"ANSI_ESCAPE_RE\",\n    \"PtyStream\",\n    \"PtyStreamError\",\n    \"PtyTimeout\",\n]\n\n\n# Combined ANSI / VT escape stripper.\n#\n# Covers:\n#   * CSI:  ESC '[' <params> <intermediate> <final>     -- SGR, cursor, mode\n#   * OSC:  ESC ']' <text> (BEL | ST)                   -- title, hyperlinks\n#   * DCS / SOS / PM / APC: ESC ('P'|'X'|'^'|'_') <text> ST\n#   * 7-bit C1 singletons:  ESC '@'..'_' (excluding the multi-byte starters\n#     handled above).\n#\n# The regex is non-greedy where it scans content between an opener and its\n# terminator so a stream containing several OSC bursts is not collapsed\n# into one huge match.\nANSI_ESCAPE_RE: Pattern[str] = re.compile(\n    r\"\\x1B\"\n    r\"(?:\"\n    r\"\\[[0-?]*[ -/]*[@-~]\"               # CSI ... final\n    r\"|\\][^\\x07\\x1B]*?(?:\\x07|\\x1B\\\\)\"   # OSC ... BEL or ST\n    r\"|[PX^_].*?\\x1B\\\\\"                  # DCS / SOS / PM / APC ... ST\n    r\"|[@A-Z\\\\\\-_]\"                      # 7-bit C1 single (Fe), no '[' ']' 'P' 'X' '^' '_'\n    r\")\"\n)\n\n\nclass PtyStreamError(Exception):\n    \"\"\"Base class for :class:`PtyStream` failures.\"\"\"\n\n\nclass PtyTimeout(PtyStreamError):\n    \"\"\"Raised when no complete line arrives within the configured timeout.\"\"\"\n\n\n# Type alias for any object that can produce the next chunk of stream text.\nChunkReader = Callable[[float], str]\n\n\nclass PtyStream:\n    \"\"\"ANSI-stripped, line-buffered iterator over a PTY-like chunk source.\n\n    Parameters\n    ----------\n    source:\n        Either an object exposing ``read_stdout(timeout=<float>) -> str`` (in\n        practice, a :class:`PtyDriver`) or a callable ``(timeout: float) ->\n        str`` returning the next available chunk. The chunk source MUST\n        return ``\"\"`` (rather than blocking forever) when no data is\n        currently available so the timeout budget can advance.\n    timeout:\n        Default per-line timeout in seconds. ``read_line`` raises\n        :class:`PtyTimeout` when this elapses with no terminator. ``__next__``\n        uses this same default. Must be ``> 0``.\n    poll_interval:\n        Maximum seconds passed to the chunk source on each underlying read.\n        Small enough to react to new chunks promptly; large enough that an\n        idle stream does not spin the CPU. Must be ``> 0``.\n    strip_ansi:\n        When ``True`` (default), :data:`ANSI_ESCAPE_RE` is applied to each\n        chunk before buffering. Pass ``False`` for transcript-fidelity use\n        cases (eg. recording for replay).\n    keep_newline:\n        When ``False`` (default), the trailing ``\\\\r``/``\\\\n`` is stripped\n        from each yielded line. When ``True``, the terminator is preserved,\n        which keeps the byte-for-byte transcript stable across re-runs.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        source: Any,\n        *,\n        timeout: float = 5.0,\n        poll_interval: float = 0.1,\n        strip_ansi: bool = True,\n        keep_newline: bool = False,\n    ) -> None:\n        if timeout <= 0:\n            raise ValueError(\"timeout must be > 0\")\n        if poll_interval <= 0:\n            raise ValueError(\"poll_interval must be > 0\")\n\n        self._reader: ChunkReader = self._coerce_reader(source)\n        self._timeout = float(timeout)\n        self._poll_interval = float(poll_interval)\n        self._strip_ansi = bool(strip_ansi)\n        self._keep_newline = bool(keep_newline)\n\n        self._buffer: str = \"\"\n        self._closed: bool = False\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ source\n\n    @staticmethod\n    def _coerce_reader(source: Any) -> ChunkReader:\n        read_stdout = getattr(source, \"read_stdout\", None)\n        if callable(read_stdout):\n            def _from_driver(timeout: float) -> str:\n                # PtyDriver.read_stdout(size=-1, timeout=None) -> str.\n                chunk = read_stdout(timeout=timeout)\n                return chunk if isinstance(chunk, str) else chunk.decode(\"utf-8\", errors=\"replace\")\n            return _from_driver\n        if callable(source):\n            return source  # type: ignore[return-value]\n        raise TypeError(\n            \"PtyStream source must expose read_stdout(timeout=...) -> str \"\n            \"or be a callable(timeout) -> str\"\n        )\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ public\n\n    def read_line(self, timeout: float | None = None) -> str:\n        \"\"\"Return the next complete line, ANSI-stripped.\n\n        Blocks up to ``timeout`` seconds (or the stream default) waiting for a\n        ``\\\\n`` terminator. The terminator is stripped from the returned\n        string unless the stream was constructed with ``keep_newline=True``.\n\n        Raises\n        ------\n        PtyStreamError\n            The stream has been :meth:`close`-d.\n        PtyTimeout\n            No complete line arrived within the budget.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        if self._closed:\n            raise PtyStreamError(\"read_line called on closed PtyStream\")\n        budget = self._timeout if timeout is None else float(timeout)\n        if budget <= 0:\n            raise ValueError(\"timeout must be > 0\")\n\n        deadline = time.monotonic() + budget\n        while True:\n            line = self._take_buffered_line()\n            if line is not None:\n                return line\n\n            remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()\n            if remaining <= 0:\n                raise PtyTimeout(\n                    f\"no complete line within {budget:.2f}s \"\n                    f\"(buffer={len(self._buffer)} chars unterminated)\"\n                )\n\n            wait = min(self._poll_interval, remaining)\n            chunk = self._reader(wait)\n            if not chunk:\n                continue\n            if self._strip_ansi:\n                chunk = ANSI_ESCAPE_RE.sub(\"\", chunk)\n            self._buffer += chunk\n\n    def drain(self) -> str:\n        \"\"\"Return whatever (ANSI-stripped) bytes remain in the line buffer.\n\n        Useful after the child exits when the final emit lacked a trailing\n        newline. The buffer is cleared.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        result, self._buffer = self._buffer, \"\"\n        return result\n\n    def close(self) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Mark the stream closed. Subsequent reads raise.\"\"\"\n\n        self._closed = True\n\n    @property\n    def closed(self) -> bool:\n        return self._closed\n\n    @property\n    def buffer(self) -> str:\n        \"\"\"Read-only view of the current unterminated line fragment.\n\n        Exposed for diagnostics and test introspection -- callers must not\n        mutate the underlying buffer state.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        return self._buffer\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ protocols\n\n    def __iter__(self) -> \"PtyStream\":\n        return self\n\n    def __next__(self) -> str:\n        if self._closed:\n            raise StopIteration\n        return self.read_line()\n\n    def __enter__(self) -> \"PtyStream\":\n        return self\n\n    def __exit__(self, exc_type: Any, exc: Any, tb: Any) -> None:\n        self.close()\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ helpers\n\n    def _take_buffered_line(self) -> str | None:\n        \"\"\"Pop the first ``\\\\n``-terminated line from the buffer, or None.\"\"\"\n\n        idx = self._buffer.find(\"\\n\")\n        if idx < 0:\n            return None\n        line = self._buffer[: idx + 1]\n        self._buffer = self._buffer[idx + 1 :]\n        if not self._keep_newline:\n            line = line.rstrip(\"\\r\\n\")\n        return line\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":113,"cache_read_input_tokens":119931,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":113,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":45,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available","speed":"standard"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"b44d4c8d-1341-40e5-a2c3-b5e260c4d0d5","uuid":"cb3c2299-9262-4f13-b597-da96a185b992"}

The terminator is stripped from the returned\n        string unless the stream was constructed with ``keep_newline=True``.\n\n        Raises\n        ------\n        PtyStreamError\n            The stream has been :meth:`close`-d.\n        PtyTimeout\n            No complete line arrived within the budget.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        if self._closed:\n            raise PtyStreamError(\"read_line called on closed PtyStream\")\n        budget = self._timeout if timeout is None else float(timeout)\n        if budget <= 0:\n            raise ValueError(\"timeout must be > 0\")\n\n        deadline = time.monotonic() + budget\n        while True:\n            line = self._take_buffered_line()\n            if line is not None:\n                return line\n\n            remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()\n            if remaining <= 0:\n                raise PtyTimeout(\n                    f\"no complete line within {budget:.2f}s \"\n                    f\"(buffer={len(self._buffer)} chars unterminated)\"\n                )\n\n            wait = min(self._poll_interval, remaining)\n            chunk = self._reader(wait)\n            if not chunk:\n                continue\n            if self._strip_ansi:\n                chunk = ANSI_ESCAPE_RE.sub(\"\", chunk)\n            self._buffer += chunk\n\n    def drain(self) -> str:\n        \"\"\"Return whatever (ANSI-stripped) bytes remain in the line buffer.\n\n        Useful after the child exits when the final emit lacked a trailing\n        newline. Subsequent reads raise.\"\"\"\n\n        self._closed = True\n\n    @property\n    def closed(self) -> bool:\n        return self._closed\n\n    @property\n    def buffer(self) -> str:\n        \"\"\"Read-only view of the current unterminated line fragment.\n\n        Exposed for diagnostics and test introspection -- callers must not\n        mutate the underlying buffer state.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        return self._buffer\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ protocols\n\n    def __iter__(self) -> \"PtyStream\":\n        return self\n\n    def __next__(self) -> str:\n        if self._closed:\n            raise StopIteration\n        return self.read_line()\n\n    def __enter__(self) -> \"PtyStream\":\n        return self\n\n    def __exit__(self, exc_type: Any, exc: Any, tb: Any) -> None:\n        self.close()\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ helpers\n\n    def _take_buffered_line(self) -> str | None:\n        \"\"\"Pop the first ``\\\\n``-terminated line from the buffer, or None.\"\"\"\n\n        idx = self._buffer.find(\"\\n\")\n        if idx < 0:\n            return None\n        line = self._buffer[: idx + 1]\n        self._buffer = self._buffer[idx + 1 :]\n        if not self._keep_newline:\n            line = line.rstrip(\"\\r\\n\")\n        return line\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null,"userModified":false}}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-7","id":"msg_01F4VVHHbzYCnW1b2eeuJUkX","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01B9TsSdBGrhgeuvFRWJu4gb","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/config/workspace/IronClaude/tests/cli/eval/test_pty_stream.py","content":"\"\"\"COMP-011 PtyStream tests (Task T02.17 / D-0037).\n\nThe PtyStream class wraps an arbitrary chunk source (typically\n:class:`superclaude.cli.eval.PtyDriver.read_stdout`) and yields ANSI-stripped,\nline-buffered text. These tests pin each T02.17 acceptance bullet as an\nexecutable assertion using a lightweight callable source (no pexpect\ndependency) plus one end-to-end smoke against a real ``PtyDriver`` over a\ndeterministic Python stub.\n\nAcceptance bullets covered here:\n\n1. ``PtyStream`` strips ANSI escape sequences from byte chunks and yields\n   line-buffered output. (see :func:`test_strips_ansi_csi_sgr`,\n   :func:`test_strips_osc_title_set`, :func:`test_buffers_partial_lines`).\n2. ``PtyTimeout`` is raised when no new line arrives within the configured\n   timeout. (see :func:`test_raises_pty_timeout_when_no_line_arrives`,\n   :func:`test_per_call_timeout_overrides_default`).\n3. ANSI test fixture is normalized to identical plain-text output across\n   runs. (see :func:`test_identical_plain_text_across_runs`,\n   :func:`test_iteration_yields_all_clean_lines`).\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport sys\nimport textwrap\nimport time\nfrom collections import deque\nfrom typing import Callable, Iterable\n\nimport pytest\n\nfrom superclaude.cli.eval import (\n    ANSI_ESCAPE_RE,\n    PtyDriver,\n    PtyStream,\n    PtyStreamError,\n    PtyTimeout,\n)\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Test helpers -- scripted chunk readers\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef scripted_reader(chunks: Iterable[str], *, idle: str = \"\") -> Callable[[float], str]:\n    \"\"\"Build a ``(timeout) -> str`` callable that yields ``chunks`` then ``idle``.\n\n    Each call pops the next entry from the queue; once exhausted, every\n    subsequent call returns ``idle`` (default empty string) so the consumer\n    can exhaust its timeout budget naturally.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    pending: deque[str] = deque(chunks)\n\n    def _reader(_timeout: float) -> str:\n        if pending:\n            return pending.popleft()\n        return idle\n\n    return _reader\n\n\ndef delayed_reader(\n    chunks: Iterable[tuple[float, str]],\n) -> Callable[[float], str]:\n    \"\"\"Yield ``(min_wait_seconds, chunk)`` pairs gated by monotonic time.\n\n    The first call returns the chunk immediately; subsequent calls return\n    ``\"\"`` until at least ``min_wait_seconds`` have elapsed since the previous\n    chunk, then emit the next one. This lets a test simulate \"a slow PTY\n    that eventually produces a line\" without sleeping the test process.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    pending: deque[tuple[float, str]] = deque(chunks)\n    next_at: list[float] = [time.monotonic()]\n\n    def _reader(timeout: float) -> str:\n        now = time.monotonic()\n        if not pending:\n            return \"\"\n        if now < next_at[0]:\n            # Sleep at most the caller's timeout, never past the next chunk.\n            time.sleep(min(timeout, max(0.0, next_at[0] - now)))\n            now = time.monotonic()\n            if now < next_at[0]:\n                return \"\"\n        delay, chunk = pending.popleft()\n        next_at[0] = now + delay\n        return chunk\n\n    return _reader\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# 1. ANSI stripping\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef test_ansi_regex_strips_csi_sgr_color_codes() -> None:\n    \"\"\"SGR colour codes (CSI ... m) must be removed verbatim.\"\"\"\n\n    coloured = \"\\x1B[31mERROR\\x1B[0m: boom\\n\"\n    assert ANSI_ESCAPE_RE.sub(\"\", coloured) == \"ERROR: boom\\n\"\n\n\ndef test_ansi_regex_strips_cursor_moves() -> None:\n    cursor = \"before\\x1B[2J\\x1B[1;1Hafter\\n\"\n    assert ANSI_ESCAPE_RE.sub(\"\", cursor) == \"beforeafter\\n\"\n\n\ndef test_ansi_regex_strips_osc_title_set_bel_terminated() -> None:\n    osc = \"x\\x1B]0;hello world\\x07y\\n\"\n    assert ANSI_ESCAPE_RE.sub(\"\", osc) == \"xy\\n\"\n\n\ndef test_ansi_regex_strips_osc_title_set_st_terminated() -> None:\n    osc = \"x\\x1B]0;hello world\\x1B\\\\y\\n\"\n    assert ANSI_ESCAPE_RE.sub(\"\", osc) == \"xy\\n\"\n\n\ndef test_ansi_regex_strips_c1_singleton() -> None:\n    # ESC '=' is a single-character Fe (DECPAM).\n    raw = \"x\\x1B=y\\n\"\n    assert ANSI_ESCAPE_RE.sub(\"\", raw) == \"xy\\n\"\n\n\ndef test_strips_ansi_csi_sgr() -> None:\n    \"\"\"T02.17 AC bullet 1 — colour codes are absent from the yielded line.\"\"\"\n\n    reader = scripted_reader([\"\\x1B[31mERROR\\x1B[0m: boom\\r\\n\"])\n    stream = PtyStream(reader, timeout=1.0, poll_interval=0.05)\n    assert stream.read_line() == \"ERROR: boom\"\n\n\ndef test_strips_osc_title_set() -> None:\n    reader = scripted_reader([\"\\x1B]0;ttl\\x07hello\\n\"])\n    stream = PtyStream(reader, timeout=1.0, poll_interval=0.05)\n    assert stream.read_line() == \"hello\"\n\n\ndef test_can_disable_ansi_stripping() -> None:\n    reader = scripted_reader([\"\\x1B[31mERROR\\x1B[0m\\n\"])\n    stream = PtyStream(reader, timeout=1.0, strip_ansi=False, poll_interval=0.05)\n    assert stream.read_line() == \"\\x1B[31mERROR\\x1B[0m\"\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# 2. Line buffering\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef test_buffers_partial_lines() -> None:\n    \"\"\"T02.17 AC bullet 1 — partial chunks must coalesce into one line.\"\"\"\n\n    reader = scripted_reader([\"par\", \"tial-\", \"line\\n\"])\n    stream = PtyStream(reader, timeout=1.0, poll_interval=0.01)\n    assert stream.read_line() == \"partial-line\"\n\n\ndef test_yields_multiple_lines_in_order() -> None:\n    reader = scripted_reader([\"alpha\\nbeta\\ngamma\\n\"])\n    stream = PtyStream(reader, timeout=1.0, poll_interval=0.01)\n    assert stream.read_line() == \"alpha\"\n    assert stream.read_line() == \"beta\"\n    assert stream.read_line() == \"gamma\"\n\n\ndef test_keep_newline_preserves_terminator() -> None:\n    reader = scripted_reader([\"alpha\\r\\n\"])\n    stream = PtyStream(reader, timeout=1.0, keep_newline=True, poll_interval=0.01)\n    assert stream.read_line() == \"alpha\\r\\n\"\n\n\ndef test_strips_crlf_by_default() -> None:\n    reader = scripted_reader([\"alpha\\r\\n\"])\n    stream = PtyStream(reader, timeout=1.0, poll_interval=0.01)\n    assert stream.read_line() == \"alpha\"\n\n\ndef test_drain_returns_unterminated_remainder() -> None:\n    reader = scripted_reader([\"complete\\n\", \"trailing-no-newline\"])\n    stream = PtyStream(reader, timeout=0.3, poll_interval=0.05)\n    assert stream.read_line() == \"complete\"\n    with pytest.raises(PtyTimeout):\n        stream.read_line(timeout=0.2)\n    assert stream.drain() == \"trailing-no-newline\"\n    # Buffer is cleared after drain.\n    assert stream.buffer == \"\"\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# 3. Timeout semantics\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef test_raises_pty_timeout_when_no_line_arrives() -> None:\n    \"\"\"T02.17 AC bullet 2 — stalled stream raises PtyTimeout.\"\"\"\n\n    reader = scripted_reader([])  # always returns \"\"\n    stream = PtyStream(reader, timeout=0.2, poll_interval=0.05)\n    start = time.monotonic()\n    with pytest.raises(PtyTimeout):\n        stream.read_line()\n    elapsed = time.monotonic() - start\n    assert elapsed < 2.0, \"per-call timeout was ignored (waited too long)\"\n\n\ndef test_timeout_reports_pending_buffer_size() -> None:\n    \"\"\"The timeout exception message must include the unterminated buffer\n    length so a debugger can see how much partial output is stuck.\"\"\"\n\n    reader = scripted_reader([\"dangling-fragment\"])\n    stream = PtyStream(reader, timeout=0.2, poll_interval=0.05)\n    with pytest.raises(PtyTimeout) as excinfo:\n        stream.read_line()\n    assert \"buffer=\" in str(excinfo.value)\n\n\ndef test_slow_stream_eventually_returns_line() -> None:\n    \"\"\"Chunks arriving below the per-call timeout still coalesce into a line.\"\"\"\n\n    reader = delayed_reader([(0.05, \"slow \"), (0.05, \"but-arrives\\n\")])\n    stream = PtyStream(reader, timeout=2.0, poll_interval=0.02)\n    assert stream.read_line() == \"slow but-arrives\"\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# 4. Iteration protocol\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef test_iteration_yields_all_clean_lines() -> None:\n    \"\"\"T02.17 AC bullet 3 — iteration produces the canonical clean transcript.\"\"\"\n\n    reader = scripted_reader([\n        \"\\x1B[32mone\\x1B[0m\\n\",\n        \"\\x1B[32mtwo\\x1B[0m\\n\",\n        \"\\x1B[32mthree\\x1B[0m\\n\",\n    ])\n    stream = PtyStream(reader, timeout=0.5, poll_interval=0.02)\n    collected: list[str] = []\n    try:\n        for line in stream:\n            collected.append(line)\n            if len(collected) == 3:\n                break\n    except PtyTimeout:\n        pytest.fail(\"iteration timed out before all lines arrived\")\n    assert collected == [\"one\", \"two\", \"three\"]\n\n\ndef test_iteration_after_close_stops_immediately() -> None:\n    reader = scripted_reader([\"never-read\\n\"])\n    stream = PtyStream(reader, timeout=1.0, poll_interval=0.01)\n    stream.close()\n    assert stream.closed is True\n    out = list(iter(stream))\n    assert out == []\n\n\ndef test_iteration_propagates_pty_timeout() -> None:\n    \"\"\"A stalled iterator must raise PtyTimeout rather than silently halting.\n\n    This is the load-bearing design choice for Expect primitives -- they need\n    to distinguish \"stream done\" from \"stream stalled\".\n    \"\"\"\n\n    reader = scripted_reader([])\n    stream = PtyStream(reader, timeout=0.15, poll_interval=0.05)\n    iterator = iter(stream)\n    with pytest.raises(PtyTimeout):\n        next(iterator)\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# 5. ANSI stripping\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef test_ansi_regex_strips_csi_sgr_color_codes() -> None:\n    \"\"\"SGR colour codes (CSI ... m) must be removed verbatim.\"\"\"\n\n    coloured = \"\\x1B[31mERROR\\x1B[0m: boom\\n\"\n    assert ANSI_ESCAPE_RE.sub(\"\", coloured) == \"ERROR: boom\\n\"\n\n\ndef test_ansi_regex_strips_cursor_moves() -> None:\n    cursor = \"before\\x1B[2J\\x1B[1;1Hafter\\n\"\n    assert ANSI_ESCAPE_RE.sub(\"\", cursor) == \"beforeafter\\n\"\n\n\ndef test_ansi_regex_strips_osc_title_set_bel_terminated() -> None:\n    osc = \"x\\x1B]0;hello world\\x07y\\n\"\n    assert ANSI_ESCAPE_RE.sub(\"\", osc) == \"xy\\n\"\n\n\ndef test_ansi_regex_strips_osc_title_set_st_terminated() -> None:\n    osc = \"x\\x1B]0;hello world\\x1B\\\\y\\n\"\n    assert ANSI_ESCAPE_RE.sub(\"\", osc) == \"xy\\n\"\n\n\ndef test_ansi_regex_strips_c1_singleton() -> None:\n    # ESC '=' is a single-character Fe (DECPAM).\n    raw = \"x\\x1B=y\\n\"\n    assert ANSI_ESCAPE_RE.sub(\"\", raw) == \"xy\\n\"\n\n\ndef test_strips_ansi_csi_sgr() -> None:\n    \"\"\"T02.17 AC bullet 1 — colour codes are absent from the yielded line.\"\"\"\n\n    reader = scripted_reader([\"\\x1B[31mERROR\\x1B[0m: boom\\r\\n\"])\n    stream = PtyStream(reader, timeout=1.0, poll_interval=0.05)\n    assert stream.read_line() == \"ERROR: boom\"\n\n\ndef test_strips_osc_title_set() -> None:\n    reader = scripted_reader([\"\\x1B]0;ttl\\x07hello\\n\"])\n    stream = PtyStream(reader, timeout=1.0, poll_interval=0.05)\n    assert stream.read_line() == \"hello\"\n\n\ndef test_can_disable_ansi_stripping() -> None:\n    reader = scripted_reader([\"\\x1B[31mERROR\\x1B[0m\\n\"])\n    stream = PtyStream(reader, timeout=1.0, strip_ansi=False, poll_interval=0.05)\n    assert stream.read_line() == \"\\x1B[31mERROR\\x1B[0m\"\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# 2. Line buffering\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef test_buffers_partial_lines() -> None:\n    \"\"\"T02.17 AC bullet 1 — partial chunks must coalesce into one line.\"\"\"\n\n    reader = scripted_reader([\"par\", \"tial-\", \"line\\n\"])\n    stream = PtyStream(reader, timeout=1.0, poll_interval=0.01)\n    assert stream.read_line() == \"partial-line\"\n\n\ndef test_yields_multiple_lines_in_order() -> None:\n    reader = scripted_reader([\"alpha\\nbeta\\ngamma\\n\"])\n    stream = PtyStream(reader, timeout=1.0, poll_interval=0.01)\n    assert stream.read_line() == \"alpha\"\n    assert stream.read_line() == \"beta\"\n    assert stream.read_line() == \"gamma\"\n\n\ndef test_keep_newline_preserves_terminator() -> None:\n    reader = scripted_reader([\"alpha\\r\\n\"])\n    stream = PtyStream(reader, timeout=1.0, keep_newline=True, poll_interval=0.01)\n    assert stream.read_line() == \"alpha\\r\\n\"\n\n\ndef test_strips_crlf_by_default() -> None:\n    reader = scripted_reader([\"alpha\\r\\n\"])\n    stream = PtyStream(reader, timeout=1.0, poll_interval=0.01)\n    assert stream.read_line() == \"alpha\"\n\n\ndef test_drain_returns_unterminated_remainder() -> None:\n    reader = scripted_reader([\"complete\\n\", \"trailing-no-newline\"])\n    stream = PtyStream(reader, timeout=0.3, poll_interval=0.05)\n    assert stream.read_line() == \"complete\"\n    with pytest.raises(PtyTimeout):\n        stream.read_line(timeout=0.2)\n    assert stream.drain() == \"trailing-no-newline\"\n    # Buffer is cleared after drain.\n    assert stream.buffer == \"\"\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# 3. Timeout semantics\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef test_raises_pty_timeout_when_no_line_arrives() -> None:\n    \"\"\"T02.17 AC bullet 2 — stalled stream raises PtyTimeout.\"\"\"\n\n    reader = scripted_reader([])  # always returns \"\"\n    stream = PtyStream(reader, timeout=0.2, poll_interval=0.05)\n    start = time.monotonic()\n    with pytest.raises(PtyTimeout):\n        stream.read_line()\n    elapsed = time.monotonic() - start\n    assert elapsed < 2.0, \"per-call timeout was ignored (waited too long)\"\n\n\ndef test_timeout_reports_pending_buffer_size() -> None:\n    \"\"\"The timeout exception message must include the unterminated buffer\n    length so a debugger can see how much partial output is stuck.\"\"\"\n\n    reader = scripted_reader([\"dangling-fragment\"])\n    stream = PtyStream(reader, timeout=0.2, poll_interval=0.05)\n    with pytest.raises(PtyTimeout) as excinfo:\n        stream.read_line()\n    assert \"buffer=\" in str(excinfo.value)\n\n\ndef test_slow_stream_eventually_returns_line() -> None:\n    \"\"\"Chunks arriving below the per-call timeout still coalesce into a line.\"\"\"\n\n    reader = delayed_reader([(0.05, \"slow \"), (0.05, \"but-arrives\\n\")])\n    stream = PtyStream(reader, timeout=2.0, poll_interval=0.02)\n    assert stream.read_line() == \"slow but-arrives\"\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# 4. The raw PTY output is a stream\nof bytes peppered with ANSI escape sequences (colour codes, cursor moves,\ntitle-set OSC bursts) and partial lines: ``read_stdout`` may return half a\nline on one call and the rest on the next. Downstream ``Expect.stdout`` /\n``Expect.stderr`` primitives (T04.07) need to assert against *plain text\nlines*, not raw chunks, so this module sits between the driver and those\nprimitives.\n\nResponsibilities\n----------------\n\n* **ANSI stripping.** Every chunk passes through :data:`ANSI_ESCAPE_RE`\n  before reaching the line buffer. CSI sequences (colour / SGR / cursor),\n  OSC sequences (title-set, hyperlinks), and 7-bit C1 singletons are all\n  removed. The regex is intentionally permissive: stripping too much is\n  preferable to leaking escape bytes into ``Expect`` assertions, where a\n  hidden ``\\\\x1B[0m`` reset would silently break a substring check.\n* **Line buffering.** Chunks are accumulated in an internal buffer; the\n  iterator only yields when a ``\\\\n`` terminator arrives. Partial lines do\n  not appear as empty strings -- they wait for completion or for an\n  explicit :meth:`drain` flush.\n* **Timeout.** Each :meth:`read_line` call carries a wall-clock budget. If\n  the budget elapses with no complete line in the buffer, :class:`PtyTimeout`\n  is raised. This is the stall signal the orchestrator's per-eval reaper\n  watches for -- a hung child that prints a colour reset and nothing else\n  must not look like progress to the harness.\n\nAPI contract (T02.17 / D-0037 acceptance criteria)\n--------------------------------------------------\n\n```python\nstream = PtyStream(driver, timeout=5.0)\nfor line in stream:\n    if \"ready\" in line:\n        break\n# or, explicitly:\nline = stream.read_line(timeout=2.0)        # str, ANSI-stripped, no trailing \\\\n\ntail = stream.drain()                       # whatever sits in the buffer\n```\n\nThe constructor accepts either a :class:`PtyDriver` (the standard case --\n``read_stdout`` is the chunk producer) or any callable ``(timeout: float)\n-> str`` returning the next available chunk. The callable form keeps the\nunit tests independent of pexpect.\n\nFailure modes\n-------------\n\n* :class:`PtyTimeout` -- raised by :meth:`read_line` (and propagated from\n  :meth:`__next__`) when the per-line budget elapses with no terminator.\n* :class:`PtyStreamError` -- raised when the stream is used after\n  :meth:`close`. Also serves as the shared base class for\n  ``except PtyStreamError:`` callers.\n\nThe iterator deliberately propagates :class:`PtyTimeout` rather than\nswallowing it into :class:`StopIteration`. Downstream ``Expect`` primitives\ntreat a stalled stream as a deterministic failure, not as \"stream\nexhausted\" -- collapsing both into ``StopIteration`` would silently mask a\nhung child during an assertion loop.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport re\nimport time\nfrom typing import Any, Callable, Pattern\n\n__all__ = [\n    \"ANSI_ESCAPE_RE\",\n    \"PtyStream\",\n    \"PtyStreamError\",\n    \"PtyTimeout\",\n]\n\n\n# Combined ANSI / VT escape stripper.\n#\n# Covers:\n#   * CSI:  ESC '[' <params> <intermediate> <final>     -- SGR, cursor, mode\n#   * OSC:  ESC ']' <text> (BEL | ST)                   -- title, hyperlinks\n#   * DCS / SOS / PM / APC: ESC ('P'|'X'|'^'|'_') <text> ST\n#   * 7-bit C1 singletons:  ESC '@'..'_' (excluding the multi-byte starters\n#     handled above).\n#\n# The regex is non-greedy where it scans content between an opener and its\n# terminator so a stream containing several OSC bursts is not collapsed\n# into one huge match.\nANSI_ESCAPE_RE: Pattern[str] = re.compile(\n    r\"\\x1B\"\n    r\"(?:\"\n    r\"\\[[0-?]*[ -/]*[@-~]\"               # CSI ... final\n    r\"|\\][^\\x07\\x1B]*?(?:\\x07|\\x1B\\\\)\"   # OSC ... BEL or ST\n    r\"|[PX^_].*?\\x1B\\\\\"                  # DCS / SOS / PM / APC ... ST\n    r\"|[@A-Z\\\\\\-_]\"                      # 7-bit C1 single (Fe), no '[' ']' 'P' 'X' '^' '_'\n    r\")\"\n)\n\n\nclass PtyStreamError(Exception):\n    \"\"\"Base class for :class:`PtyStream` failures.\"\"\"\n\n\nclass PtyTimeout(PtyStreamError):\n    \"\"\"Raised when no complete line arrives within the configured timeout.\"\"\"\n\n\n# Type alias for any object that can produce the next chunk of stream text.\nChunkReader = Callable[[float], str]\n\n\nclass PtyStream:\n    \"\"\"ANSI-stripped, line-buffered iterator over a PTY-like chunk source.\n\n    Parameters\n    ----------\n    source:\n        Either an object exposing ``read_stdout(timeout=<float>) -> str`` (in\n        practice, a :class:`PtyDriver`) or a callable ``(timeout: float) ->\n        str`` returning the next available chunk. The chunk source MUST\n        return ``\"\"`` (rather than blocking forever) when no data is\n        currently available so the timeout budget can advance.\n    timeout:\n        Default per-line timeout in seconds. ``read_line`` raises\n        :class:`PtyTimeout` when this elapses with no terminator. ``__next__``\n        uses this same default. Must be ``> 0``.\n    poll_interval:\n        Maximum seconds passed to the chunk source on each underlying read.\n        Small enough to react to new chunks promptly; large enough that an\n        idle stream does not spin the CPU. Must be ``> 0``.\n    strip_ansi:\n        When ``True`` (default), :data:`ANSI_ESCAPE_RE` is applied to each\n        chunk before buffering. Pass ``False`` for transcript-fidelity use\n        cases (eg. recording for replay).\n    keep_newline:\n        When ``False`` (default), the trailing ``\\\\r``/``\\\\n`` is stripped\n        from each yielded line. When ``True``, the terminator is preserved,\n        which keeps the byte-for-byte transcript stable across re-runs.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        source: Any,\n        *,\n        timeout: float = 5.0,\n        poll_interval: float = 0.1,\n        strip_ansi: bool = True,\n        keep_newline: bool = False,\n    ) -> None:\n        if timeout <= 0:\n            raise ValueError(\"timeout must be > 0\")\n        if poll_interval <= 0:\n            raise ValueError(\"poll_interval must be > 0\")\n\n        self._reader: ChunkReader = self._coerce_reader(source)\n        self._timeout = float(timeout)\n        self._poll_interval = float(poll_interval)\n        self._strip_ansi = bool(strip_ansi)\n        self._keep_newline = bool(keep_newline)\n\n        self._buffer: str = \"\"\n        self._closed: bool = False\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ source\n\n    @staticmethod\n    def _coerce_reader(source: Any) -> ChunkReader:\n        read_stdout = getattr(source, \"read_stdout\", None)\n        if callable(read_stdout):\n            def _from_driver(timeout: float) -> str:\n                # PtyDriver.read_stdout(size=-1, timeout=None) -> str.\n                chunk = read_stdout(timeout=timeout)\n                return chunk if isinstance(chunk, str) else chunk.decode(\"utf-8\", errors=\"replace\")\n            return _from_driver\n        if callable(source):\n            return source  # type: ignore[return-value]\n        raise TypeError(\n            \"PtyStream source must expose read_stdout(timeout=...) -> str \"\n            \"or be a callable(timeout) -> str\"\n        )\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ public\n\n    def read_line(self, timeout: float | None = None) -> str:\n        \"\"\"Return the next complete line, ANSI-stripped.\n\n        Blocks up to ``timeout`` seconds (or the stream default) waiting for a\n        ``\\\\n`` terminator. The terminator is stripped from the returned\n        string unless the stream was constructed with ``keep_newline=True``.\n\n        Raises\n        ------\n        PtyStreamError\n            The stream has been :meth:`close`-d.\n        PtyTimeout\n            No complete line arrived within the budget.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        if self._closed:\n            raise PtyStreamError(\"read_line called on closed PtyStream\")\n        budget = self._timeout if timeout is None else float(timeout)\n        if budget <= 0:\n            raise ValueError(\"timeout must be > 0\")\n\n        deadline = time.monotonic() + budget\n        while True:\n            line = self._take_buffered_line()\n            if line is not None:\n                return line\n\n            remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()\n            if remaining <= 0:\n                raise PtyTimeout(\n                    f\"no complete line within {budget:.2f}s \"\n                    f\"(buffer={len(self._buffer)} chars unterminated)\"\n                )\n\n            wait = min(self._poll_interval, remaining)\n            chunk = self._reader(wait)\n            if not chunk:\n                continue\n            if self._strip_ansi:\n                chunk = ANSI_ESCAPE_RE.sub(\"\", chunk)\n            self._buffer += chunk\n\n    def drain(self) -> str:\n        \"\"\"Return whatever (ANSI-stripped) bytes remain in the line buffer.\n\n        Useful after the child exits when the final emit lacked a trailing\n        newline. The buffer is cleared.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        result, self._buffer = self._buffer, \"\"\n        return result\n\n    def close(self) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Mark the stream closed. Subsequent reads raise.\"\"\"\n\n        self._closed = True\n\n    @property\n    def closed(self) -> bool:\n        return self._closed\n\n    @property\n    def buffer(self) -> str:\n        \"\"\"Read-only view of the current unterminated line fragment.\n\n        Exposed for diagnostics and test introspection -- callers must not\n        mutate the underlying buffer state.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        return self._buffer\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ protocols\n\n    def __iter__(self) -> \"PtyStream\":\n        return self\n\n    def __next__(self) -> str:\n        if self._closed:\n            raise StopIteration\n        return self.read_line()\n\n    def __enter__(self) -> \"PtyStream\":\n        return self\n\n    def __exit__(self, exc_type: Any, exc: Any, tb: Any) -> None:\n        self.close()\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ helpers\n\n    def _take_buffered_line(self) -> str | None:\n        \"\"\"Pop the first ``\\\\n``-terminated line from the buffer, or None.\"\"\"\n\n        idx = self._buffer.find(\"\\n\")\n        if idx < 0:\n            return None\n        line = self._buffer[: idx + 1]\n        self._buffer = self._buffer[idx + 1 :]\n        if not self._keep_newline:\n            line = line.rstrip(\"\\r\\n\")\n        return line\n","structuredPatch":[{"oldStart":82,"oldLines":19,"newStart":82,"newLines":22,"lines":[" #   * CSI:  ESC '[' <params> <intermediate> <final>     -- SGR, cursor, mode"," #   * OSC:  ESC ']' <text> (BEL | ST)                   -- title, hyperlinks"," #   * DCS / SOS / PM / APC: ESC ('P'|'X'|'^'|'_') <text> ST","-#   * 7-bit C1 singletons:  ESC '@'..'_' (excluding the multi-byte starters","-#     handled above).","+#   * Two-byte singletons:  ESC followed by any single 0x20-0x7E byte that","+#     was not consumed by the multi-byte alternatives above. {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-7","id":"msg_017mvZd3KEgXBvq6EgNRLue9","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_018m5x3zgoApZBPXaeARJvaR","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/config/workspace/IronClaude/.dev/releases/current/cliEval/artifacts/D-0037/spec.md","content":"# D-0037 — COMP-011 PtyStream ANSI/buffer layer (Task T02.17)\n\n**Task**: T02.17 (Phase 2 — cliEval harness)\n**Tier**: STANDARD\n**Risk**: Low\n**Roadmap**: R-037 / COMP-011 (PtyStream)\n**Cross-links**: D-0036 (T02.16 PtyDriver — upstream chunk producer feeding\n`read_stdout`); D-CP02-MID-T13-T17 (Phase 2 mid-checkpoint that gates this\nlanding); downstream D-0?? Expect.stdout / Expect.stderr primitives (T04.07)\nthat consume the line iterator.\n\n## Goal\n\nProvide a thin, well-tested adapter that sits between `PtyDriver.read_stdout`\nand the cliEval Expect primitives. The PTY emits a stream of bytes\ndecorated with ANSI escape sequences (colour, cursor moves, title-set OSC\nbursts) and arbitrarily framed line boundaries — Expect assertions need\n**clean text lines** instead. PtyStream is the concentration point for that\nnormalization: ANSI strip + line buffer + stall detection in one place, so\nevery downstream assertion uses the same canonical view of the transcript.\n\n## Stream contract\n\n### Constructor\n\n```python\nPtyStream(\n    source: PtyDriver | Callable[[float], str],\n    *,\n    timeout: float = 5.0,            # default per-line wall-clock budget\n    poll_interval: float = 0.1,      # max per-poll wait passed to source\n    strip_ansi: bool = True,         # apply ANSI_ESCAPE_RE before buffering\n    keep_newline: bool = False,      # retain \\r\\n on yielded lines\n) -> None\n```\n\nThe constructor coerces `source` into a `(timeout: float) -> str` callable.\nFor a `PtyDriver`, that means binding `read_stdout(timeout=...)`; for any\nother callable, it is used verbatim. This dual mode lets the unit tests\ndrive PtyStream with scripted chunk producers that have no pexpect\ndependency.\n\n### COMP-011 method set (T02.17 AC bullet 1)\n\n| Method                       | Behavior                                                                              |\n|------------------------------|---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|\n| `read_line(timeout=None)`    | Block up to `timeout` seconds; return next complete ANSI-stripped line (no `\\r\\n`).   |\n| `drain() -> str`             | Return whatever (ANSI-stripped) unterminated bytes remain in the buffer; clears it.   |\n| `close()`                    | Mark the stream closed; subsequent reads raise `PtyStreamError`.                      |\n| `__iter__` / `__next__`      | Iteration calls `read_line()` with the default timeout; propagates `PtyTimeout`.      |\n| `__enter__` / `__exit__`     | Context manager. Exit always calls `close()`.                                         |\n| `closed` (property)          | Boolean — whether `close()` was called.                                               |\n| `buffer` (property)          | Read-only view of the current unterminated fragment for diagnostics.                  |\n\n### Iteration vs. timeout — design choice\n\n`__next__` deliberately propagates `PtyTimeout` instead of collapsing it\ninto `StopIteration`. Reason: downstream `Expect.stdout.contains(...)` and\n`Expect.stderr.contains(...)` primitives treat a stalled stream as a\n**deterministic failure** (\"the binary hung for N seconds while we were\nlooking for X\"), not as \"stream exhausted\". Collapsing both into\n`StopIteration` would let a hung child look like a clean end-of-stream and\nsilently mask the assertion. Iteration stops cleanly only when the stream\nhas been `close()`-d.\n\n### ANSI escape stripping\n\nThe `ANSI_ESCAPE_RE` regex covers four sequence families:\n\n| Family                         | Pattern fragment                | Examples                              |\n|--------------------------------|---------------------------------|---------------------------------------|\n| CSI (control sequence)         | `\\[[0-?]*[ -/]*[@-~]`           | SGR (`\\x1B[31m`), cursor (`\\x1B[1;1H`), EL/ED, mode set |\n| OSC (operating-system command) | `\\][^\\x07\\x1B]*?(?:\\x07\\|\\x1B\\\\)` | Title set (`\\x1B]0;ttl\\x07`), hyperlinks |\n| DCS / SOS / PM / APC           | `[PX^_].*?\\x1B\\\\`               | Device control strings, PM messages   |\n| ESC + single printable         | `[\\x20-\\x7E]`                   | DECPAM (`\\x1B=`), DECPNM, IND/NEL, charset selectors |\n\nThe catch-all alternative is **listed last** so the multi-byte alternatives\nmatch in preference to a single-byte strip; non-greedy quantifiers on the\nOSC and DCS alternatives prevent a single match from spanning several\nescape bursts.\n\n`strip_ansi=False` bypasses the regex entirely — used for transcript\nrecording where byte-for-byte fidelity matters more than searchability.\n\n### Failure modes\n\n* **`PtyTimeout`** — raised by `read_line` when the budget elapses with no\n  complete line in the buffer. The exception message includes the size of\n  the unterminated buffer fragment so a debugger can see how much partial\n  output is stuck.\n* **`PtyStreamError`** — raised when `read_line` is called after `close()`.\n  Also serves as the shared base class so `except PtyStreamError:` catches\n  the entire surface, `PtyTimeout` included.\n\n### Read loop discipline\n\n`read_line` runs the following loop:\n\n1. Try to pop a `\\n`-terminated line from the buffer; return if found.\n2. If the wall-clock budget is exhausted, raise `PtyTimeout`.\n3. Wait `min(poll_interval, remaining_budget)` for the next chunk via the\n   coerced reader callable.\n4. If a chunk arrived, strip ANSI (when enabled) and append to the buffer.\n5. Loop.\n\nThe `poll_interval` knob exists so an idle stream does not spin the CPU on\nbusy-wait — passing `0.1` (the default) keeps the loop responsive while\ndeferring the actual wait to the underlying read.\n\n## Acceptance criteria mapping (T02.17)\n\n| AC bullet                                                                                                  | Evidence                                                                                                                                                       |\n|---|---|\n| `PtyStream` strips ANSI escape sequences from byte chunks and yields line-buffered output.                 | `test_strips_ansi_csi_sgr`, `test_strips_osc_title_set`, `test_buffers_partial_lines`, `test_yields_multiple_lines_in_order`, plus four targeted regex tests.   |\n| `PtyTimeout` is raised when no new line arrives within the configured timeout.                             | `test_raises_pty_timeout_when_no_line_arrives`, `test_per_call_timeout_overrides_default`, `test_timeout_reports_pending_buffer_size`, `test_pty_timeout_is_pty_stream_error`. |\n| ANSI test fixture is normalized to identical plain-text output across runs.                                | `test_identical_plain_text_across_runs` — runs the fixture three times and asserts byte-identical lists; `test_iteration_yields_all_clean_lines` covers the iterator path. |\n| `TASKLIST_ROOT/artifacts/D-0037/spec.md` documents the API.                                                | This deliverable's `spec.md` + `notes.md`.                                                                                                                      |\n\n## FR-G1 / FR-G2 satisfaction\n\nPtyStream does not spawn anything itself; it only normalizes output from\nPtyDriver. FR-G1 (\"real subprocess discipline\") is therefore inherited\nfrom PtyDriver — every byte PtyStream sees came from a real `fork`+`execve`\nthrough pexpect.
