Expect.stdout / Expect.stderr primitives (T04.07)\nthat consume the line iterator.\n\n## Goal\n\nProvide a thin, well-tested adapter that sits between `PtyDriver.read_stdout`\nand the cliEval Expect primitives. The PTY emits a stream of bytes\ndecorated with ANSI escape sequences (colour, cursor moves, title-set OSC\nbursts) and arbitrarily framed line boundaries — Expect assertions need\n**clean text lines** instead. PtyStream is the concentration point for that\nnormalization: ANSI strip + line buffer + stall detection in one place, so\nevery downstream assertion uses the same canonical view of the transcript.\n\n## Stream contract\n\n### Constructor\n\n```python\nPtyStream(\n    source: PtyDriver | Callable[[float], str],\n    *,\n    timeout: float = 5.0,            # default per-line wall-clock budget\n    poll_interval: float = 0.1,      # max per-poll wait passed to source\n    strip_ansi: bool = True,         # apply ANSI_ESCAPE_RE before buffering\n    keep_newline: bool = False,      # retain \\r\\n on yielded lines\n) -> None\n```\n\nThe constructor coerces `source` into a `(timeout: float) -> str` callable.\nFor a `PtyDriver`, that means binding `read_stdout(timeout=...)`; for any\nother callable, it is used verbatim. This dual mode lets the unit tests\ndrive PtyStream with scripted chunk producers that have no pexpect\ndependency.\n\n### COMP-011 method set (T02.17 AC bullet 1)\n\n| Method                       | Behavior                                                                              |\n|------------------------------|---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|\n| `read_line(timeout=None)`    | Block up to `timeout` seconds; return next complete ANSI-stripped line (no `\\r\\n`).   |\n| `drain() -> str`             | Return whatever (ANSI-stripped) unterminated bytes remain in the buffer; clears it.   |\n| `close()`                    | Mark the stream closed; subsequent reads raise `PtyStreamError`.                      |\n| `__iter__` / `__next__`      | Iteration calls `read_line()` with the default timeout; propagates `PtyTimeout`.      |\n| `__enter__` / `__exit__`     | Context manager. Exit always calls `close()`.                                         |\n| `closed` (property)          | Boolean — whether `close()` was called.                                               |\n| `buffer` (property)          | Read-only view of the current unterminated fragment for diagnostics.                  |\n\n### Iteration vs. timeout — design choice\n\n`__next__` deliberately propagates `PtyTimeout` instead of collapsing it\ninto `StopIteration`. Reason: downstream `Expect.stdout.contains(...)` and\n`Expect.stderr.contains(...)` primitives treat a stalled stream as a\n**deterministic failure** (\"the binary hung for N seconds while we were\nlooking for X\"), not as \"stream exhausted\". Collapsing both into\n`StopIteration` would let a hung child look like a clean end-of-stream and\nsilently mask the assertion. Iteration stops cleanly only when the stream\nhas been `close()`-d.\n\n### ANSI escape stripping\n\nThe `ANSI_ESCAPE_RE` regex covers four sequence families:\n\n| Family                         | Pattern fragment                | Examples                              |\n|--------------------------------|---------------------------------|---------------------------------------|\n| CSI (control sequence)         | `\\[[0-?]*[ -/]*[@-~]`           | SGR (`\\x1B[31m`), cursor (`\\x1B[1;1H`), EL/ED, mode set |\n| OSC (operating-system command) | `\\][^\\x07\\x1B]*?(?:\\x07\\|\\x1B\\\\)` | Title set (`\\x1B]0;ttl\\x07`), hyperlinks |\n| DCS / SOS / PM / APC           | `[PX^_].*?\\x1B\\\\`               | Device control strings, PM messages   |\n| ESC + single printable         | `[\\x20-\\x7E]`                   | DECPAM (`\\x1B=`), DECPNM, IND/NEL, charset selectors |\n\nThe catch-all alternative is **listed last** so the multi-byte alternatives\nmatch in preference to a single-byte strip; non-greedy quantifiers on the\nOSC and DCS alternatives prevent a single match from spanning several\nescape bursts.\n\n`strip_ansi=False` bypasses the regex entirely — used for transcript\nrecording where byte-for-byte fidelity matters more than searchability.\n\n### Failure modes\n\n* **`PtyTimeout`** — raised by `read_line` when the budget elapses with no\n  complete line in the buffer. The exception message includes the size of\n  the unterminated buffer fragment so a debugger can see how much partial\n  output is stuck.\n* **`PtyStreamError`** — raised when `read_line` is called after `close()`.\n  Also serves as the shared base class so `except PtyStreamError:` catches\n  the entire surface, `PtyTimeout` included.\n\n### Read loop discipline\n\n`read_line` runs the following loop:\n\n1. Try to pop a `\\n`-terminated line from the buffer; return if found.\n2. If the wall-clock budget is exhausted, raise `PtyTimeout`.\n3. Wait `min(poll_interval, remaining_budget)` for the next chunk via the\n   coerced reader callable.\n4. If a chunk arrived, strip ANSI (when enabled) and append to the buffer.\n5. Loop.\n\nThe `poll_interval` knob exists so an idle stream does not spin the CPU on\nbusy-wait — passing `0.1` (the default) keeps the loop responsive while\ndeferring the actual wait to the underlying read.\n\n## Acceptance criteria mapping (T02.17)\n\n| AC bullet                                                                                                  | Evidence                                                                                                                                                       |\n|---|---|\n| `PtyStream` strips ANSI escape sequences from byte chunks and yields line-buffered output.                 | `test_strips_ansi_csi_sgr`, `test_strips_osc_title_set`, `test_buffers_partial_lines`, `test_yields_multiple_lines_in_order`, plus four targeted regex tests.   |\n| `PtyTimeout` is raised when no new line arrives within the configured timeout.                             | `test_raises_pty_timeout_when_no_line_arrives`, `test_per_call_timeout_overrides_default`, `test_timeout_reports_pending_buffer_size`, `test_pty_timeout_is_pty_stream_error`. |\n| ANSI test fixture is normalized to identical plain-text output across runs.                                | `test_identical_plain_text_across_runs` — runs the fixture three times and asserts byte-identical lists; `test_iteration_yields_all_clean_lines` covers the iterator path. |\n| `TASKLIST_ROOT/artifacts/D-0037/spec.md` documents the API.                                                | This deliverable's `spec.md` + `notes.md`.                                                                                                                      |\n\n## FR-G1 / FR-G2 satisfaction\n\nPtyStream does not spawn anything itself; it only normalizes output from\nPtyDriver. {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-7","id":"msg_01M9n1zP7pViu5bvsCheiSex","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01RCcV6aVZwknHPXa7zrrHUS","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/config/workspace/IronClaude/.dev/releases/current/cliEval/artifacts/D-0037/notes.md","content":"# D-0037 — Implementation notes (Task T02.17)\n\n## Why the ANSI catch-all uses `[\\x20-\\x7E]` instead of just Fe\n\nThe original draft only matched 7-bit C1 (Fe) single-byte escapes —\n`[@A-Z\\\\-_]`. That regex passed the SGR / CSI / OSC tests but failed on\n`\\x1B=` (DECPAM, Set Application Keypad), an Fp-range Fs single-byte\nescape `claude` emits during keypad-mode reset. The fix broadens the\nsingle-byte alternative to the full `[\\x20-\\x7E]` printable range and\nkeeps it as the **last** alternative so multi-byte sequences (CSI, OSC,\nDCS) still bind their full payload before the catch-all has a chance.\n\nThe catch-all is intentionally permissive. ANSI streams in the wild\ncontain a long tail of vendor-specific singletons (DEC's\n`\\x1B7`/`\\x1B8` cursor save/restore, charset selectors `\\x1B(B`, etc.).\nEnumerating every legitimate final-byte value would invite hidden gaps\nwhen a new sequence appears in a `claude` minor release. Over-stripping\na stray printable byte after ESC is a far cheaper failure than leaving\na colour-reset in the buffer where it can break `Expect.contains` —\nthat bias is explicit in the spec.\n\n## Why the iterator raises `PtyTimeout` instead of `StopIteration`\n\nThe first draft swallowed `PtyTimeout` into `StopIteration` so a\n`for line in stream:` loop would exit cleanly on a stall. That is the\n**wrong** default for the Expect primitives: a stalled child looks\nidentical to a finished one, and assertions like `Expect.stdout.contains`\nsilently pass-by-omission when the child hangs.\n\nThe spec change: iteration propagates the timeout. The cost is that a\nnaive caller doing `for line in stream:` must wrap in\n`try / except PtyTimeout:` if they want graceful termination — but\nthat's the **correct** call site for that decision. Callers that want\nthe \"iterate forever\" semantics call `read_line()` directly and decide\ntheir own per-line budget.\n\n`close()` is the only way to make iteration stop cleanly. The context\nmanager wires this up: `with PtyStream(driver) as stream:` always\ncloses on scope exit.\n\n## Why `read_line(timeout=0)` is rejected\n\n`pexpect.read_nonblocking(timeout=0)` already exists for \"drain whatever\nis buffered now\"; the equivalent at the PtyStream layer would be\n`stream.drain()`. We make this distinction enforceable by rejecting\n`timeout=0` outright — callers who want a non-blocking poll use the\nexplicit method instead of an overloaded `read_line(0)` call. This\nmatches the strict-arg policy in the rest of the cliEval module\n(`PtyDriver.__init__` rejects `default_timeout <= 0` for the same\nreason).\n\n## Why `poll_interval` defaults to 100 ms\n\nThe orchestrator runs up to 15 evals in parallel (NFR-PERF1, T02.15).\nA `poll_interval` of `0.1` keeps each idle stream's CPU footprint\nnegligible (one syscall per 100 ms × 15 streams = ~150 wake-ups/s,\nunder 1 % of a core on a typical dev host) while still responding to\nnew output within a tenth of a second — well below human perception\nand inside the timing tolerance of every Expect assertion in the\nroadmap. Tighter intervals (`0.01`) are available for slow-tick tests\nthat want to confirm coalescing without the wall-clock cost; the\ntests in `test_pty_stream.py::test_slow_stream_eventually_returns_line`\nexercise this path.\n\n## Why the chunk-source coercion accepts both methods and callables\n\nThe standard call site is `PtyStream(driver)` where `driver` is a\n`PtyDriver`. But the unit tests want to drive PtyStream with scripted\nchunks that have no pexpect dependency — and the eventual `Expect`\nshim (T04.07) may wrap the driver in additional layers (think\n`MockDriver` for record/replay, `BoundedDriver` for time-stop tests).\n\nThe coercion is therefore: if `source` has a `read_stdout` method, use\nthat. Otherwise, if `source` is callable, treat it as the chunk\nproducer directly. The unit tests use `scripted_reader` and\n`delayed_reader` helpers built on `collections.deque` to feed\ndeterministic chunk sequences without any I/O.\n\n## Why `drain()` exists separately from `read_line()`\n\nA common failure mode in PTY-driven evals: the child emits its final\noutput without a trailing newline (e.g. `--version` prints `1.2.3` and\nexits). Without `drain()`, the trailing fragment is stuck in the\nbuffer forever — `read_line` will keep timing out because there's no\n`\\n`, even though the child has exited and there is nothing more to\nread.\n\n`drain()` is the explicit escape hatch: after the child exits, call\n`drain()` once to flush whatever fragment is left. The fragment is\nreturned ANSI-stripped (consistent with the rest of the buffer) and\nthe internal buffer is reset to `\"\"`. Tests pin this contract via\n`test_drain_returns_unterminated_remainder`.\n\n## Why we expose `buffer` as a read-only property\n\nDiagnostic value. When an Expect assertion fails because the child\nsent the right text but never a newline, the assertion site needs to\nknow what's pending. Exposing `stream.buffer` lets the cliEval\nartifact writer (Phase 4) attach the unterminated fragment to the\nfailure record — without it, the only way to see the pending bytes\nwould be to monkeypatch the regex or grep the raw PTY transcript.\n\nThe property returns a plain string snapshot; mutation of the returned\nvalue cannot reach the internal buffer because Python strings are\nimmutable. There is no setter.\n\n## Vendoring + import strategy\n\nPtyStream's only third-party-shaped dependency is the regex it builds\nin `ANSI_ESCAPE_RE`; everything else uses `re`, `time`, and\n`collections` from the stdlib. There is no vendored regex library\nbecause the catch-all + family alternatives fit in five short\npatterns. If a future suite needs Unicode VT-220 sequences (4-byte\nfinal bytes), we can extend the catch-all to `[\\x20-\\xFF]` without\ndisturbing callers.\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":3305,"cache_read_input_tokens":142797,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":3305,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":46,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available","speed":"standard"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"b44d4c8d-1341-40e5-a2c3-b5e260c4d0d5","uuid":"ab3b5a89-2dde-4c9e-b52c-14c88087321c"}
